[In the] third place, where authorities are seized that deputies do not actually possess, just as the **Synodal Churches** <term>Eglises Synodales: Regional associations of churches within the Reformed tradition that sent representatives to Synods.</term> usurp illegitimate powers that are by no means in conformity with the word of God. In the fifth place, where nearly the same excesses occur as in the **Synods**. In the sixth place, where—when someone wishes to push a matter through quietly and by conspiracy <note>original: "cabale," referring to secret political or ecclesiastical maneuvering.</note>—they arrange to be deputized themselves, or they obtain deputies favorable to their own designs and interests.

To prove all these things, one only has to read the documents cited above, and especially to calculate the expenses of the **Classes** <term>Classes: Regional governing bodies in the Reformed Church, similar to a presbytery, consisting of ministers and elders from a specific district.</term>, and to scrutinize the conduct of the deputies during these meetings, along with the little good that results from them. This was seen in the most recent ones at **Middelburg**, where they left behind only traces of their great expenses and fine dining for entire months on the one hand, and on the other, dismal monuments of their injustice and violence, similar to those of the **Inquisition**.

*Thirdly, the same corruption is seen in the Consistorial Assemblies.* <term>Assemblées Consistoriales: The local governing body of a Reformed church, made up of the minister, elders, and deacons.</term> In the first place, because the **Ministers** immediately assume an air of empire and domination—contrary to the word of God—over the **Elders** and **Deacons**. # Declaration

<page-num>330</page-num>
<header>Declaration</header>

to verify all these things, which are common in the **Walloon Consistories** <term>Consistoires Vualons: The local governing councils of the Walloon (French-speaking) Reformed churches in the Netherlands.</term>, one has only to read **their Acts** <note>Referring to the official records and minutes of the church council meetings.</note>, and to open one's eyes to their conduct and their ways of acting; as well as to cast a glance upon most of the persons who compose them, to see what kind of people they are, and can be, being usually composed of persons similar to those we have just described.

**Fourthly, the Corruption of these same Walloon Assemblies** appears in those called **Ecclesiastical** <term>Eclesiastiques: Here referring to the full congregational gatherings for worship, rather than just the leadership meetings.</term>, that is to say **the Body of an entire Church**; firstly, in that one sees them to be lukewarm and even cold on workdays, even in places where they are most populous. Secondly, they are lazy in coming to the scripture reading, to the singing of praise, and to the prayers. Thirdly, in that there is very little respect and attention given to the word of God, and to all the **Exercises of Piety** <term>Exercices de Pieté: Formal acts of worship including prayer, scripture reading, and psalm singing.</term>. These are ordinarily interrupted by levity, chatter, immodesty, and what is more, insolence. Fourthly, in that one sees a great vanity appearing there in pompous clothes, in lace, gold and silver, pearls and precious stones on many persons, whether of high or low status. Fifthly, in that sleepers, chatterers, and even the immodest are ordinarily—

<meta>catchword: ment (continued as "ment souferts" / "tolerated")</meta>

---

<page-num>331</page-num>
<header>Christian.</header>

—tolerated, and those who are censured in other Temples are admitted and welcomed into theirs. Sixthly, and above all, in that they are even profaned and turned from **Houses of prayer** <note>A biblical reference to Matthew 21:13, "My house shall be called the house of prayer."</note> and places of repentance into marketplaces or the filth of a theater, where vanity is displayed, luxury appears, and a strange confusion is observed.

**Fifthly, the Corruption of these same Assemblies** is visible **in three principal things**: the **first** is **the literal practice of Liturgies** recited by habit and by method, for ten, twenty, and thirty years—always the same, and always said without great fruit, without attention, and by some resemblance to the way of praying of either the Gentiles or the Jews <note>The author is criticizing "rote prayer," comparing it to non-Christian or pre-Christian rituals that he considers spiritually dead.</note>, to which one is obliged to be attached, and to hold fast under penalty—if one deviates from it—of being judged as committing a great crime, of passing for a rebel against orders, rash, and even impious; and at the very least insulting to the Author of the Liturgy, to the Liturgy itself, and almost to the **Spirit of God**. To pray without it is to make oneself suspect of being a **Fanatic**, an **Enthusiast**, and at the very least a **Libertine** <term>Fanatique, Enthousiaste, Libertin: These were 17th-century pejoratives for religious radicals who claimed direct inspiration from the Holy Spirit (Enthusiasts) or who rejected moral and ecclesiastical laws (Libertines).</term>: a strange and very constraining thing, opposed to **Christian liberty**, to the maxims, and to the practices of the Prophets and the Apostles, whom we see never restricted themselves to a common method or a literal form of prayer.

# Declaration

<page-num>332</page-num>
<header>Declaration</header>
<meta>catchword: love</meta>

The second significant thing about these Assemblies is the **Ministry of the Word** <term>Administration de la Parole: The formal preaching and teaching of the Bible during a church service.</term>, in which all these abuses occur: First, that the **Ministers** <note>The ordained pastors of the congregation.</note> perform it as rarely and as little as they can themselves, usually employing as many foreigners as present themselves, or young **Candidates** <term>Proposans: Divinity students or graduates who have been licensed to preach but not yet fully ordained to a specific church.</term> whom they are quite happy to employ. Second, in that they discharge this duty perfunctorily when they perform their charge themselves, whether simply to get it over with, or merely to fill the hour destined for a **Sermon** <note>original: "Predication"</note>. Third, preaching with an air and in a fashion that is not very moving, hardly speaking to the heart, and their discourses lacking the **anointing of the Holy Spirit** <note>The "unction" or spiritual power believed to come from divine inspiration rather than mere oratorical skill.</note>, who should be speaking through them. Fourth, **Catechizing** <term>Catechisans: The practice of teaching the fundamentals of faith through a system of questions and answers, vital for the religious education of the youth.</term> very little, or doing so only superficially, not taking the trouble to adapt themselves to the capacity of simple folk and children. Fifth, delivering the Word even less in people's houses and in their conversations, whether active or passive; how much less in the rooms of the sick where they only go when forced, and recite only certain **rote scripts** <term>Rolets: Literally "little scrolls"; refers to formulaic, pre-written speeches or prayers recited from memory without genuine feeling.</term> of exhortations and prayers applicable to all sorts of afflicted people. Sixth, showing very little zeal in correcting and rebuking vices, especially those of the great and the rich, whose threats they fear,

<column-break/>

<page-num>333</page-num>
<header>Christian Declaration</header>
<meta>catchword: little</meta>

[and whose] flattery they love, and what is more, their feasts.

Likewise regarding the reading of the Word of God in these same Assemblies, we see first that it is done without being explained in any way, even though several chapters in a row are covered, and **Doctrinal and Moral subjects** are proposed in them that are difficult to understand and capable of giving rise to just difficulties regarding Faith and Morals. Second, that on the part of the listeners as well, it is heard without reverence, without attention, and without profit. Third, that it is often interrupted by their noise or their chatter. Fourth, that one hardly sees anyone moved by it, its reading and its hearing having turned into a custom, and everyone being used to setting little store by it.

The third significant thing in these same Assemblies is the **Administration of the Sacraments**, in which there are also several abuses. First, in that in the administration of **Baptism**, people are admitted as **Sponsors and Guarantors** <term>Pleges & Cautions: Godparents who were legally and spiritually bound to ensure the child was raised in the faith.</term> whose <margin>Note Well</margin> ignorance, negligence, and lack of zeal sufficiently prove that they cannot fulfill the promises they make. Second, in that in the same administration of Baptism, they are content to read a long **Liturgy** <note>The prescribed form of public worship and ritual.</note>, to which

# Declaration

<page-num>334</page-num>

<meta>catchword: of</meta>

...few people are attentive, and whose repetition turns into habit and boredom. Thirdly, in that within it, solemn Ecclesiastical oaths are given and received, which are known well enough to be illusory and made rashly. Fourthly, in that it seems everything there is done, and well done, provided that the matter and the form <term>The "matter" (the physical element, like water) and "form" (the specific sacramental words) are the essential components traditionally required for a sacrament to be considered valid.</term> are maintained in words and sprinkling <note>A reference to the ritual of Baptism.</note>, without taking much trouble to ensure the participants are properly instructed as they should be. Fifthly, in that much vanity is tolerated there among the Rich, in pompous linens or cloths, and in the finery of Persons who bring shame to the simplicity of the Sacrament.

But there are still much greater Abuses in the *Administration* of the other [sacrament], which is that of *the Lord's Supper* <note>original: "la Cene"</note>, to which, in the first place, many Children are admitted who are truly children, not so much in age as in knowledge and maturity of spirit. Secondly, in that all sorts of Persons are admitted by the Walloon Ministers <note>The pastors of the French-speaking Reformed churches, often located in the Netherlands.</note> based on answers learned by heart to the questions of a manual, for which they could not provide a reason—and much less for their Faith, if they were questioned faithfully.

In the third place, in that *the vain and the worldly* are received there without renouncing their worldliness and vanity; and what is more

<column-break/>

<page-num>335</page-num>
<header>Christian.</header>
<meta>catchword: mories</meta>

scandalous People involved in gambling, swearing, and debauchery are not Excluded at all, especially if they are rich and of high status. Fourthly, in that a great number of people are allowed to participate whose Doctrine and Morals are unknown, and whose Amendment <note>The "reformation" or improvement of one's life and behavior following repentance.</note> of life has never been proven. In the fifth place, in that many approach *the table of the body and blood of the Lord* who know neither how to discern it, nor how to examine themselves <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 11:28-29, which warns against taking Communion in an "unworthy manner."</note>, so Ignorant and poorly Instructed are they. Sixthly and finally, the little fruit produced by the Lord's Supper proves that it is not eaten well; and that instead of producing grace, it produces judgment.

There are within these same *Walloon Ecclesiastical Assemblies* some other Abuses still, which we cannot approve, such as the *Blessings of Marriages* between Persons little capable of keeping the promises they make there to live in *Faith*, in *Piety*, *and in Christian holiness*. Secondly, the same *Blessings* are accompanied by a display of vanity, in sumptuous clothes, in finery, and in other entirely worldly and Pagan ornaments, which profane what Scripture calls *Holy*. In the third place, we find it no less Strange that in the Temples or places of Ecclesiastical Assembly there are *Paintings*, *and Ar-* <note>Likely "Architecture" or "Altars," though the text cuts off.</note>

# Declaration

<page-num>336</page-num>
<header>Declaration</header>
<meta>catchword: rité (referring to "autorité" on the next page)</meta>

...[memo]ries with Spurs and Helmets hung in Monuments as much out of vanity as out of mourning; and what is more, Images of Fauns, Satyrs, naked Bacchus, Men, Women, Sirens, and all sorts of Animals. <note>The author is criticizing the presence of aristocratic heraldry and pagan mythological imagery inside Christian churches, which was common in some wealthy Dutch and Walloon congregations of the 17th century.</note>

We find it no less Strange that benches are covered in mourning; and that for entire Years they are kept stretched there as a mark of worldly mourning, as contrary to Christian humility as it is to *Faith*, and to the Word of God.

These are, for the most part, what the Walloon Ecclesiastical Assemblies are; and the Corruptions that their Synod constantly suffers in their midst, without ever attempting to bring a remedy, despite whatever complaints have been brought: so much is it afraid either of deserting its Temples <note>Meaning the loss of congregation members and their financial support.</note>, or of Irritating vanity and pride: preferring much more to retain it than to drive it out; and at the very least to tolerate it, rather than to provoke it.

*But the third Corruption of the Walloon Synod, which is that of its Government,* provides us with many other subjects for complaint, and makes it such that we can no more bear it than we can, in some way, the Papal [government]; since it holds much of its air, which we do not hesitate to call *Tyrannical* among us.

The first of the *Abuses of the Walloon Synod in its conduct, is that of the absolute autho-*

<column-break/>

<page-num>337</page-num>
<header>Christian.</header>
<sig>P</sig>
<meta>catchword: On (One)</meta>

-rity <note>original: "autorité"</note> that it has taken, and that it arrogates to itself every day over the Churches, over the Consistories <term>The Consistory is the local governing body of a Reformed church, made up of the minister and elders.</term>, and over all their public and private Members: indeed, like Tyrants, as soon as they have power in hand, they subjugate the freest cities as soon as those cities have agreed to give them ever so little, and have consented to support them; thus *the Walloon Synod*, from a voluntary and free concert of the Churches, has made for them a bit and a bridle to restrain them, however much it strikes and pricks them; and it has taken as an Imperial and Monarchical right what was Christian Republican, and even in some way Ecclesiastically Democratic. <note>The author argues that the church should be governed collectively by its members (a "Republic") rather than by a centralized, top-down authority (the Synod).</note>

It is thus that the Pope and his Conclave <term>The college of Cardinals that advises the Pope and elects new ones.</term>, for whom the Eastern and Western Churches once had a voluntary deference, made of it an obligation, and drew from it a right to rule with an iron rod <note>original: "commander à baguete," literally "to command with a rod/wand," implying absolute or autocratic control.</note>, to call cases to their tribunal, to judge them, to give them Laws, and to regulate them according to their will. So too does one see the Synod, just like the *Vatican*, draw everything to itself and take cognizance of it, and like the Pope, use these imperious words: *We command, We Ordain, We have Ordained and Decreed, We Cite, We Judge, etc. The Company Judges, Decrees, Ordains, etc.*—all words *of Empire, of Monarchy, of full power, of absolute Authority, and of pure and good Pleasure.* <note>original: "bon Plaisir," a technical legal term used in French royal edicts (e.g., "car tel est notre bon plaisir") to signify that the law stems solely from the monarch's will.</note>

The author compares the Synod's centralized authority to that of the Pope and the Vatican, arguing it has destroyed the church's original "republican" and "democratic" spirit.</summary>

# Christian Declaration

<page-num>338, 339</page-num>
<header>Christian Declaration.</header>

One knows it well enough by the New **Decrees** <note>original: "Arrets"</note> that they make. First, they are contrary to the Ancient ones. Second, they are contrary in many things to themselves and to modern precedents. Third, they are made for the most part without foundation in the *Word of God*, even *beyond it*, and against it often enough. Fourth, they are made under penalty of grievous *Ecclesiastical Censures and Synodal thunderbolts*, similar almost to those of *Rome* or of the *Inquisition*: the *Recourse to the Secular Arm* <note>The "Secular Arm" refers to the use of civil government and police power to enforce church rulings or punish dissenters.</note> not being lacking.

The second *Abuse of the Walloon Synod in its Conduct and its Government is the Tyranny it Exercises over the Pastors.* First, by obliging them to sign its *so-called Walloon confession*: although it has not been corrected and purified according to the Synod of Dordrecht <note>The Synod of Dort (1618–1619) was a major international assembly that settled the Dutch Reformed Church's stance on predestination and sought to standardize doctrine.</note>. Second, by constraining them again to sign the *Discipline* which was not signed before; and which is not, nor can be, absolutely observed in more than thirty points <note>original: "chefs," meaning chapters or articles of church law</note>. Third, by binding them to the *Liturgies* and to the formulas: even though they have the gift of instructing and of praying just as well and as usefully, and even with more application and fruit. Fourth, by taking from them the liberty of the *Prophetic Exercise* <note>A practice based on 1 Corinthians 14 where members of the congregation or junior clergy would interpret scripture under supervision; it was seen as a way to cultivate new preaching talent.</note> established and ordained by St. Paul, and recommended by Ancient and National Synods. Fifth, by taking from them

<column-break/>

the liberty of Writing—equal to that of speaking and preaching the Word—and not permitting them to bring to light a prayer or an
<margin>N. B.</margin>
Advice of Piety without its [the Synod’s] consent, which is impossible to obtain if one is not its Friend.

The Third Abuse in matters of Government is in that *the same Synods arrogate to themselves a Strange Empire and absolute Authority over whole Churches*, and over the bodies of the Consistories <note>The Consistory is the local governing council of a Reformed church, made up of the pastor and the elders.</note>, as well as over their own. First, by calling most of their cases of any importance to itself and to its sovereign judgment. Second, by quashing very easily their Acts or sentences as it pleases, and even treating them from on high as subjects or vassals. Third, by receiving, whenever it sees fit, the direct Complaints of individuals against their [Consistory] bodies; although no Complaint to them, nor denial of justice on their part, nor even Appeal has preceded; just as *the recent Synods of Leiden, Vlissingen, and Naarden* <note>Fleſſingue and Naerden in the original.</note> received those of some individuals *against the Entire body of the Consistory of the Walloon Church of Middelburg*. Fourth, by receiving from individuals against these Bodies *defamatory, injurious, and calumnious libels*; reading them and hearing them read with attention and pleasure, as it did in the said Synods with those of the said complainants.

# Christian Declaration

<page-num>340, 341</page-num>
<header>Christian Declaration.</header>

fifthly, by not delivering justice regarding such **Libels**, insults, and calumnies: even though it was requested to do so, as it was by the **Church of Middelburg** <note>A significant Walloon church in the capital of Zeeland, known for its strict adherence to reform principles.</note>, which has summoned several people in vain. # Declaration

<page-num>342</page-num>
<header>Declaration</header>

[Naarden] has usurped, and the boldness it has taken to suspend **Mr. Jean de Labadie** <note>Jean de Labadie (1610–1674) was a famous French mystic and pastor who eventually broke away from the Reformed church to found the "Labadists."</note>, Pastor of the Walloon Church <note>The "Walloon" churches were French-speaking Reformed congregations in the Netherlands, often founded by refugees from France and the Spanish Netherlands.</note> of Middelburg; a man not only of age, being nearly sixty years old, but of good and long service in the Churches of God; known and renowned everywhere for his knowledge, for his talents, for his Piety; and whose writings and books, given in number to the public, bear witness to his **Pure Doctrine** and good life; just as the beautiful and numerous attestations from **entire Church Bodies**, **Synods**, **Classes** <note>A "Classis" is a regional governing body in the Reformed tradition, similar to a presbytery.</note>, **Academies**, **Principalities**, and even **Republics** bear witness. And [they have dared] to bar him from the **Holy Supper** <note>The Eucharist or Communion.</note>, and from the Pulpit, and from all the functions of his legitimate and authentic Pastorate, without having given any cause; at least if the Synod that suspended him had wished to be just to him, or even to be merely just to him with rigor.

<margin>N.B. <note>Latin: *Nota Bene*, meaning "Note Well."</note></margin> In effect, it could not suspend him unless he had committed one of those crimes that **Ecclesiastical Discipline** calls **Infamous** and **Ignominious**, and **punishable by the Magistrate**, of which the said Pastor is neither convicted nor criminal. It could not suspend him without the Body of his Church, and without its Advice and its Consent; without that of his **Consistory** <note>The local governing council of the individual church.</note> to which it should have first referred him. It could not suspend him unless he had been threatened, heard, convicted, and found in contempt, and that

<column-break/>

<page-num>343</page-num>
<header>Christian.</header>

all the forms required in such a case had been observed. And yet, against all form and against all order, against all custom and all Law, the Walloon Synod of **Naarden**, without having either just cause or full and required authority, suspended him without hearing him and without convicting him; without notifying his Church or his Consistory; and even against all their desire and their design, to their great oppression and to his own; and undoubtedly less to his regret than to theirs, and to that of all good people.

The Pretexts that the Walloon Synod took to suspend him, as it sets them out itself in its ninth Article, are six in number, of which the **first** is to have calumniated **Louis Wolzogen** <note>A colleague and rival of Labadie who held more rationalist views on Scripture.</note>, Walloon Minister of Utrecht, by denouncing his book entitled *Of the Interpreter of Scripture* <note>Original title: "De l'interprète de l'Écriture."</note> as being full of **Pelagianism**, **Papism**, and **other errors** at the Synod of **Vlissingen**, and having maintained it as such at the **Synod of Naarden** <note>"Pelagianism" is a theological doctrine viewed as heresy because it minimizes the role of divine grace; "Papism" was a derogatory term for Roman Catholic influence.</note>. The **second** is to have been Rebellious toward this Synod, having refused to subscribe to the Synodal approval of the said book and to make a **reparation of honor**, or formal apology, to the said **Wolzogen**. The **third** is to have been found a liar and a disguiser of the truth in the Publication or distribution of some book of his since the settlement made at Vlissingen, of which he has not sufficiently cleared himself. The **fourth** is to have broken the said settlement by the printing of a book

neither the honor of his Church nor the purity of the Faith could allow him to act otherwise.

All these things did not prevent the Synod—swept away by violence as much as by jealousy, and full of resentment because *Mr. de Labadie* aimed to reform it through his preachings and his books—from suspending him in his absence, against all order, without his knowledge and that of his Consistory <note>The local governing body of a Reformed church, composed of the minister and elders.</note> and his Church. Nor did it stop them from blackening his name as much as they could through *seven or eight of their Articles*, in which they tried to wither him and make him *infamous to the whole World*, making these articles public and having them printed, as well as those of *their Classis* <note>A regional assembly of ministers and elders within the Reformed tradition.</note> afterward, in both languages. In this, the Synod discredited itself far more than him, and showed its cruelty in persecuting his innocence both at *Naarden* and elsewhere.

This Church is not the only one that this Synod has ill-treated or oppressed; but *in striking the Pastor, it has struck the entire flock*, and primarily the most significant sheep, who can well be called Pastors: namely, *the Elders and Deacons, the leaders of that same Church*. The Synod not only had them *suspended* but, what is more, *deposed* by a small *Classis of only eight Ministers in total*, and ministers of very small Churches, mostly young men, the eldest not being
<meta>catchword: being</meta>

any more skilled or less reckless than they were.

Has anything similar ever been seen in the Reformation, since the Churches have called themselves *Reformed?* And would anyone ever have imagined it, especially happening as it did? Never did the *Walloon Church of Middelburg* have (generally speaking) *such a body of Consistory*, composed of members whose intelligence, prudence, and integrity are known and publicly acknowledged in Middelburg; and whose good life, zeal, and pastoral care were the joy and consolation of their *sheep*. And yet, *a small Classis of eight insignificant and young ministers dared to suspend and depose* all these individuals, without saying a word to their Church, or seeking its advice, and much less its approval? They dared to have its pulpit invaded even on a Sunday by *Henry du Moulin*, their rightful cast-off <note>The text suggests Du Moulin had been previously rejected or dismissed by the Middelburg congregation.</note> and their unjust enemy. Subsequently, the entire body of the Church having cried out against such a high-handed outrage and *this tyrannical and violent act*, the Classis dared—notwithstanding the opposition of the Church and that of the Magistrates (who, to avoid the consequences of these violent and entirely tyrannical proceedings, had for a time closed the church building and suspended its assemblies)—to pursue them elsewhere, and in a corner of a Flemish church, serving as a Consistory room—

# Declaration

<page-num>356</page-num>

Tyrannical Ecclesiastical Government, to another Head of this same conduct, which is the fifth concerning the Laws and the orders that the Walloon Synod never ceases to impose every day on individual Churches: For example, *that they may only choose Walloon Pastors, and call upon foreigners only if they have come to be heard, and if they have signed their Walloon confession, even though it is accused of error. That they cannot present or claim to have Candidates <note>The original term is *proposans*, referring to students who have completed their studies and are authorized to preach but not yet fully ordained.</note> admitted unless they have lived an entire year in these Provinces at their own expense. That the Churches shall be required to send their Deputies to the Synod twice a year—those from Zeeland <note>A maritime province in the southwestern Netherlands.</note> crossing the Sea even in dangerous and inconvenient times, at great expense, long absence, and at the risk or neglect of the sick and the poor; and after all, usually for matters of nothing. That they must keep the Candidates the Synod gives them for four or five years without changing, because it so pleases the Synod, and because they are under its yoke. That they must pay the taxes and levies of the Synod, which disposes of their funds as it wishes, and even distributes them poorly; and that they should not expect to have the Classical Deputies <note>Representatives of the *classis*, the regional administrative body of the church.</note> give an account of their expenses, since the Ministers must be believed on their word, or on their simple notes.*

Finally, one only has to leaf through their re-
<meta>catchword: gîtres (registers)</meta>

<column-break/>

Synodal registers to see in how many points they constrain the Churches and their members from day to day: *even to the point of arrogating the Authority to cut them off without telling them anything, and suspending them without their Advice from a Lord’s Supper <note>The original term is *Cène*, referring to Holy Communion.</note> over which they have no proper right. This has caused several Good Elders and Leaders of the Churches to say and write: That in a short time the Synods will become the Churches themselves, or will put themselves in their place. That they will make and unmake their Consistories <note>The local governing body of a Reformed church, composed of the pastor and elders.</note> at their whim, judge all their causes, and draw to themselves all their Authority and all their rights. That, in a word, they will become the Pope and the Cardinals, or at the very least the Primates and the Bishops, arrogating Power to themselves at the very least; since they cannot have the Croziers <note>The hooked staffs carried by bishops as a symbol of pastoral office.</note> and the Revenues.*

It is for this very reason that a modern Author did not fear to say in a treatise of his on Synods, which he recently published: *That they gave reason to take them, in their corruption and in their way of acting, for the Image of the Beast <note>A biblical reference to the Book of Revelation (13:11–15), often used in Protestant polemics to describe perceived ecclesiastical tyranny or the Antichrist.</note>: so much do they seem to speak like it, when they do not speak nor act like the Assembly of Jerusalem.* Indeed, they only speak like the Beast regarding their Orders and their Traditions. They command, they ordain like it, under penalty of censure and indignation; they threaten, they thunder, and even fulminate, they
<meta>catchword: con-</meta>

# 358 Declaration

...they cut and sever with the spiritual sword; being very grieved that they do not have, and cannot wield, the temporal power <note>The author distinguishes between "spiritual" authority (church discipline) and "temporal" authority (civil or physical force), suggesting the Synod leaders wish they could use the latter.</note>. And finally they dominate, they fight, and they conquer, they reign, and they triumph in proportion as the *Beast*, or the *Woman* mounted upon it <note>A reference to the apocalyptic imagery in the Book of Revelation (Chapters 17–18), traditionally used by Protestants to symbolize the corruption of the Roman Church.</note>, remains *Queen* there, *fighting the saints and overcoming them*: believing that she will *never be defeated, and will never suffer any harm*; But it is for Kings to take heed of this, and to see that they have given it too much Power, which they must eventually take away, and even pluck from it the feathers that make it fly too high.

All the things alleged regarding these three great *Corruptions of the Walloon Synodal Ecclesiastical body*, namely *Corruption in Doctrine; Corruption in societies or Assemblies; and Corruption in Conduct*, or in Government, being clear and constant to us. It should not be a surprise if *We, Faithful and Humble Members of the Church of God and of Jesus Christ, have Declared and do Declare* that we cannot in conscience have Ecclesiastical Communion with the said *Walloon Synodal Body*, as it is and remains corrupted, and in the event that it remains such and does not Reform itself in the said three *significant points*: For which, in proportion, *All Evangelical and Reformed Protestants have Protested and do Protest* that they could not, and cannot in conscience, have communion in proportion with the Pope,

<meta>catchword: &</meta>

<column-break/>

# Christian Declaration 359

and Papism, the Councils or Synods of Trent, and of Florence, of Basel, of Constance <note>These are major Roman Catholic church councils held between the 15th and 16th centuries which the Reformers rejected.</note>, and several other preceding ones; as well as with the *Inquisition* and the *Hierarchy*, or *Episcopal Monarchy*; Protesting once again that we make the said *Declaration* only for the glory of God, Father, Son, and Holy Spirit; For the support of the pure Religion, Faith, and Doctrine, truly Evangelical, Reformed, and Christian; for the maintenance of the *Purity, Authority, Clarity*, and other true qualities of the Scripture; For *sound and holy liberty of conscience*; And to avoid falling back into *Babylonian or Roman captivity, or some State similar to theirs*.

In witness whereof and of all the things written above, as we are ready to prove and uphold them as most true, We have signed below with our hearts, and more than with our pens, in the presence of God, and for the discharge of our Souls, in the Disposition and Resolution to sign them (if it were necessary) with our blood. Amen.

->SIR,<-

IT has already been eight days since the *Gazettes* <note>17th-century news-sheets or early newspapers that reported on social and political events.</note> informed us that your *Curious History* of the life of Labadie was about to appear in public; this must undoubtedly be a piece as entertaining for the variety of its subject matter as it is of great benefit for disabusing the public of the too-favorable opinion it had conceived of this little man. I believed that, since the work is not yet finished, I ought to inform you of a piece which well deserves to take its place there: it is that he lives in Amsterdam in a new fashion, having changed the black of the Jesuits & the white of the Carmelites <note>Labadie had a volatile religious career, moving from the Jesuit order to the Carmelites, then to the Reformed Church, before founding his own sect.</note>—which he wore one after the other—for the gray of the *Conventuals* <note>A branch of the Franciscan order; the author is likely mocking Labadie's shifting allegiances by comparing his new communal life to a Franciscan friary.</note>. For he has established in his home a kind of convent, more certainly composed of both sexes than that illustrious lady <note>Likely a reference to Anna Maria van Schurman (1607–1678), a famous scholar and polymath who shocked Europe by giving up her academic pursuits to join Labadie’s communal sect.</note> who threw herself into his faction, had previously been <note>The text breaks off mid-word here; "au-" and the catchword "trefois" form "autrefois" (formerly/in the past).</note>

...as previously stated, etc. <note>original: "ut prædictum est, &c."</note> and it is further ordained that these statutes and ordinances shall be held as inviolable by all brothers of the **Abbey** <note>original: "Abbatia"</note>. No member of this community, regardless of rank or office, shall be permitted to act in contradiction to the aforementioned rules.

### Article V: Of the Penalties for Non-Compliance

If any monk should presume <note>In legal terms, to "presume" means to take it upon oneself to act without authority or against established law.</note> to violate these institutions <term>institutions: The foundational laws or established customs of a religious order.</term>, he shall be subject to the following discipline:

1.  **First Offense:** A private rebuke by the **Abbot** <note>The elected head and spiritual father of the monastery.</note>.
2.  **Second Offense:** Public confession during the meeting of the **Chapter** <term>Chapter: The daily assembly of monks for the reading of the Rule and the discussion of community business.</term>.
3.  **Third Offense:** Separation from the common table and, if necessary, more severe ecclesiastical censures.

->Done and enacted in our Chapter House<-

In the year of our Lord, one thousand two hundred and fifty-five, on the feast of Saint John the Baptist. <margin>original: "In festo S. Johannis Bapt."</margin>

<summary>This page concludes the monastic statutes by detailing the penalties for monks who fail to follow the rules, following the repetitive printing error found on page 262. It establishes a three-strike system of discipline overseen by the Abbot.</summary>

<keywords>monastic discipline, penalties, Chapter House, Abbot, statutes, ecclesiastical law, Saint John the Baptist</keywords>

### Article VI: Of the Admission of Novices

It is further ordained and established that no person shall be admitted to the probation of the **Novitiate** <term>Novitiate: A mandatory trial period for individuals seeking to join a religious order, designed to test their suitability before they take final vows.</term> unless he has first attained the age of twenty years. Furthermore, any candidate must be found to be of sound body and mind, and must be free from any prior legal obligation or the bond of marriage.

1.  **The Petition:** The candidate shall first present his humble request for entry to the **Prior** <note>The second-in-command of the monastery, who assists the Abbot in daily administration and spiritual oversight.</note> and the assembled brothers in the Chapter.
2.  **The Year of Trial:** If accepted, he shall remain in the house of the novices for one full year <note>original: "per unum annum integrum"</note>, during which time he shall be diligently instructed in the **Rule of Saint Benedict** <note>The foundational set of instructions for Western monastic life, written in the 6th century, emphasizing obedience, humility, and work.</note>.
3.  **The Profession:** After the completion of this year, if he remains steadfast in his purpose and is approved by the majority of the brothers, he may be permitted to make his solemn profession of vows in the presence of the community.

->Confirmed by the Bishop of the Diocese<-

<margin>original: "Confirmatio Episcopi"</margin>

In witness of these statutes, we have caused the seal of our office to be affixed to these presents. Given on the octave <term>octave: The eight-day period following a major religious feast, often used as a formal period for concluding legal or ecclesiastical business.</term> of the feast of Saint John the Baptist, in the year of grace one thousand two hundred and fifty-five.

<summary>This page outlines the qualifications and procedural steps for entering the monastery as a novice. It mandates a minimum age of twenty and a one-year trial period focused on the Rule of Saint Benedict before final vows can be taken.</summary>
